UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
212
C–9.5: Comprehends stories and identifies characters, storyline and 
what the author wants to say
Activity 1
Reading Stories 
Material required
Story books, puppets, projector or screen.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 6 years
Organise a reading time session with children. Narrate short 
stories from Panchatantra, Jataka Tales, etc., for 3–4 minutes, 
6 – 7 minutes, and 10 minutes depending up on the age of the 
child. Any medium can be used for storytelling like storybooks, 
projectors, picture books, or a digital medium. This can be done 
during free play/language time or during concept development 
time. Encourage the children to listen to the story attentively. At 
the end of the story, ask the children to name the characters in the 
story. Read out the title of the story to start with.
Variations
Teacher can ask the child questions about the plot, characters or 
props used in the story. Talk about different endings to the story 
and ask the children to say what they think/feel about it. Children 
can be asked to see picture story books on different themes/topics.
Other competencies 
addressed
Develops communication, vocabulary development, develops self-
confidence, understands and appreciates emotions, recognises 
different emotions, interacts comfortably, shows cooperation, 
responds positively, explores and plays with one’s own voice. 
What can be observed?
Observe children during reading time or story time. Identify 
children who are not attentive in listening and those who are fully 
engrossed. Also observe the children who can describe the story, 
and those who feel connected with the characters of the story.


Vigyanmaya Kosha
213
Activity 2
Sequencing the Story
Material required
Picture cards of various stories, picture cards with one line 
sentence on them, story cards with only narration of stories in 
words without pictures (for 5 – 6 years).
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Conduct a session during story time or circle time. Give a set of 
picture cards to each child and ask her/him to arrange the picture 
cards provided to them in sequence as per the story. You can also 
ask them to identify the characters of the story through picture 
cards. 
4 – 5 years
Conduct a session during story time or circle time. Give a set of 
picture cards with one line sentence on them, to each child and 
ask her/him to arrange the cards in sequence as per the story. You 
can also ask them to identify the characters of the story through 
picture cards and identify the storyline and talk about the same 
with their friends. Talk about a different ending to the story
5 – 6 years
Conduct a session during story time or circle time. Give a set of 
story cards with only narration (without pictures) to each child 
and ask her/him to arrange the cards in sequence as per the 
story. You can also ask them to identify the characters of the story 
through cards, identify the storyline and what the author wants to 
say through this story, that is, the meaning or the message that 
we learn from the story.
Other competencies 
addressed
Communication with peers or adults around them, vocabulary 
development, develops self-confidence, language development, 
sequential thinking. Differentiates sounds and sound patterns, 
recognises different emotions, interacts comfortably, creates simple 
songs, knows and uses words effectively, develops phonological 
awareness, develops a fit and flexible body, interacts with other 
children and adults.
What can be observed?
Observe children during reading time or story time. See if they 
are trying to make different sequence of the story events than the 
expected sequence, trying out different arrangements of the story 
cards, or children who are not able to understand the correct 
sequence of the story. For them, the teacher should try different 
methods, for example, narrating a short simple story with voice 
modulation with props or organising a role play/puppet show, 
etc., instead of using story cards.

214
Activity 3
Story Week
Material required
Puppets of different kinds — stick puppets, glove puppets and finger 
puppets as per the stories and background scenes related to the 
story. Set up the classroom with a different story theme weekly by 
displaying the scenes or props related to that story.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Plan a day in a week for narrating new stories. On the other days, 
any known story can be narrated. It can be narrated during free 
play time, during circle time or while building a concept. Organise 
the classrom with displays related to any story and ask children to 
guess which story they are going to listen by looking at the displays.
4 – 5 years
Conduct a story activity as mentioned above during free play or circle 
time. Ask children to identify the theme for the day by observing the 
displays and props. Ask them to identify the story, the characters 
from that story and the storyline for the same.
5 – 6 years
Conduct a story activity as mentioned above during free play time, 
circle time or concept building time. Ask children to identify the 
theme for the day by observing the displays and props. Ask them 
to identify the story, the characters from that story, the storyline of 
the same and what the author wants to say through this story, that 
is, the meaning or the message that we learn from the story.
Variations
Ask questions to the child about the story/plot, characters or props 
used in the story. Talk about a different ending to the story and ask 
children to say what they think/feel about it.
Other competencies 
addressed
Communication with peers or adults around them, vocabulary 
development, develops self-confidence, differentiates sounds and 
sound patterns, recognises different emotions, interacts comfortably, 
creates simple songs, knows and uses words effectively, develops 
phonological awareness, develops fit and flexible body, interacts 
with other children and adults, shows precision and control while 
working with hands.
What can be observed?
Observe children during story time. Identify children who are 
attentive, not attentive, able to reproduce the story in the correct 
sequence, and children who need extra help to understand the 
story.


Vigyanmaya Kosha
215
C–9.6: Narrates short stories with clear plot and characters
Activity 1
Let us Enjoy Story Time
Material required
Big story books, small story books with pictures, vegetables, flash cards
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Story box: Make the children sit in a circle. The teacher can keep 
a box with some objects in it. They can decorate the box to make it 
interesting for the children. Ensure there is scope for developing/
narrating stories on those objects. Objects should be familiar to the 
children. For example, toy cat, doll, bag, pencil. Keep these objects 
hidden in the box. Ask the child to take out one object from the box 
and narrate the story on it in one’s own language.
4 – 5 years
•	 Make the children sit in a circle and narrate a story to them. 
Encourage them to predict what they think will happen next in 
the story.
•	 This is a good indicator of the understanding of the story among 
children. Once children are familiar with the story, ask them to 
identify the characters.
•	 Narrate a few stories in a similiar way. Tell the children a brief 
storyline of a story and encourage them to name the story.
•	 Ask simple and indirect questions to the children. For example, 
in the story ‘Hungry Caterpillar’, which fruits did the hungry 
caterpillar eat on Monday and Friday? How many vegetables did 
the caterpillar eat? How many fruits did the caterpillar eat?
5 – 6 years
•	 Teacher can ask complicated and indirect questions to the 
children. For example, in the story ‘Hungry Caterpillar’, how did 
the caterpillar turn into butterfly?
•	 Narrate a story to the children by laying down story cards. Jumble 
the story cards and encourage children to retell the story in their 
own words by arranging the story cards in the correct sequence.
Variations
Story week celebration: Display the posters and props of different 
stories on each day. Let the child guess the story, narrate the story, 
do activities related to the story and take the props home to narrate 
the story in front of family members.
Other competencies 
addressed
Communication with peers, social development, vocabulary 
enhancement, differentiates sounds and sound patterns, recognises 
different emotions, interacts comfortably, creates simple songs, 
knows and uses words effectively, develops phonological awareness, 
develops a fit and flexible body, interacts with other children and 
adults.
What can be observed?
Observe the style of language, clarity of pronunciation, confidence, 
verbal and facial expressions of children.

Activity 2
Narrate A Story Using Props
Material required

Age group
How to conduct the activity?

Divide the children into groups. Distribute the story flash cards to

4 – 5 years

as per the given picture card, for example: picture of a dog with a

5 – 6 years
•	 Divide the class into groups of four to five children. Each group 
will think and narrate a familiar story with a different ending. 
•	 Divide the class into groups and give each group a familiar story

the story using the props.

Activity 3

Material required

Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years

•	 Narrate a story to the class. Take a basket and fill it with props,

UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
218
C–9.7: Knows and uses enough words to carry out day-to-day 
interactions effectively and can guess the meaning of new 
words by using existing vocabulary
Activity 1
Who am I?
Material required
Children can bring any object from their home
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Organise circle time with the children. Encourage them to bring 
an object from home. Let them sit in a circle. Give each child an 
opportunity to speak a few lines about the object to their friends.
4 – 5 years
•	 Let the children close their eyes. Take an object and hide it 
behind your back. Describe the object and ask the children to 
guess what it is. Use riddle phrases such as: ‘I am long, I am 
shiny, I am used to eat/sip with, what am I?’ (a spoon).
•	 Give as many clues as needed until they guess the object. Once 
the children have guessed the object correctly, let them describe 
it in their own words.
5 – 6 years
•	 Let the children pick the odd one out from the given words. 
For example; apple, peach, banana, fish (fish is the odd word, 
others are fruits).
•	 Encourage the children to expand their vocabulary by talking 
about different objects, themes, etc.
Variations
•	 The teacher can prompt the kids by giving words or ideas to talk 
about.
•	 Encourage the children to observe their surroundings when 
taken out on a nature walk or a field trip.
Other competencies 
addressed
Communication with peers or adults around them, vocabulary 
development, develops self-confidence, public-speaking skills, 
awareness about the environment around them, differentiates 
objects and things, interacts comfortably, cooperates, responds 
positively, sorts objects into groups, identifies patterns, understands 
oral instructions.
What can be observed?
•	 Observe children interacting during circle time, free play, group 
activities, outdoor play.
•	 Observe the vocabulary and new words used by children.


Vigyanmaya Kosha
219
Activity 2
Picture Talk
Material required
Picture of a park, railway station, hospital, marketplace, etc.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Conduct a session during circle time by showing the picture of 
a park. Ask the children to observe the picture from left to right 
and name the objects, things and characters that they see in the 
picture. 
4  –  5 years
Conduct a session during circle time. Show the picture of a park or 
any other theme to the children. Ask them to observe the picture 
from left to right and describe the scene in their own words. For 
example, ‘an old lady is sitting on a bench, two children are playing 
with the ball, ‘a girl and a boy are playing on the see-saw’, etc. 
Encourage them to use the right vocabulary.
5 – 6 years
Conduct a session during circle time. Show the picture of a park or 
any other theme to the children. Ask them to observe the picture 
from left to right and describe the scene in their own words by 
connecting it with their own past experiences of a similar place. For 
example– when they had visited the park, what all did they see, do 
or experience there? The teacher can prompt the children by giving 
words or ideas to talk about.
Other competencies 
addressed
Communication with peers or adults around them, vocabulary 
development, develops self-confidence, public-speaking skills, 
observation skills, and awareness about the environment around 
them.
What can be observed?
Observe children using routine or new vocabulary during circle 
time, free play, group activities and outdoor play.


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
220
Activity 3
Word Game
Material required
Board, marker/chalk, duster
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Scavenger hunt
•	 This is a simple word game that will not only help build vocabulary 
but also the power of observation.
•	 Hide objects in the classroom, and let the children find these 
objects. Whenever an object is found, the child has to show it to 
everyone and tell them what it is and describe it. 
•	 Conduct a word game with the children by drawing pictures on 
the board. Ask each child to identify the picture, whether it is a 
fruit, flower, vegetable, animal, or bird.
•	 Keep common objects/pictures of sweet and sour items and ask 
the children to name them and tell the names of things having 
similar taste that they have tasted before.
4 – 5 years
Crossword: Make a square grid with hidden words. Give a list of 
words (words children are familiar with) to be searched from the 
grid. Encourage the children to fill in the missing letters to create a 
meaningful word. They can make a sentence using that word. 
5 – 6 years
•	 Let the children sit in a circle. Give them a short four letter word, 
e.g. bake. The children in turn have to replace one letter at a 
time, turn by turn to create a new word. They will notice that it 
will make a completely different word from that of the original. 
Some words will not make sense.
•	 Let children observe the nonsensical words also. Example: 
Bake – Fake, Make; Male – Tale; Tape – Cape; Care – Rare and the 
list goes on.
Variations
•	 Give the children picture cards of various fruits, flowers or 
vegetables. Ask them to name fruits, flowers or vegetables shown 
to them.
•	 Design a crossword puzzle and encourage the children to 
complete it.
Other competencies 
addressed
Interacts comfortably to different thoughts, responds positively, 
identifies and extends simple patterns, socio-emotional development.
What can be observed?
Observe the children acquiring new vocabulary during circle time, 
free play, group activities and outdoor play.


GOAL
10
C–10.1: Develops phonological awareness and blends phonemes/ 
syllables into words and segments words into phonemes/ 
syllables in L1 
Activity 1
Catch the Sound 
Material required
Rhymes/song audio, worksheet, rattle, picture cards.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Provide an opportunity to listen to the sounds of nature (rainfall, 
wind blowing, chirping of birds, etc.).
•	 Use fingers of hands to do actions on rhymes and jingles, for 
example—  “मछली जल की रानी है”
•	 Provide opportunities to speak and listen to others.
4 – 5 years
•	 Let the children identify the beginning sound of their name. For 
example— ‘पलक’, ‘प’
•	 Listen to the songs/rhymes and help them identify the rhyming 
words in the poems/rhymes (for example, “I went  to a house 
and saw a mouse”).
•	 Organise a puppet show related to animal, birds and so on.
CG–10: Children develop fluency in reading and writing 
in Language 1 (L1)
The activities for emergent reading, emergent writing and bonding with 
books have been developed with an assumption that the first language/ 
language of instruction in Balvatika is the home language or the regional 
language.


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
222
5 – 6 years
•	 Encourage free conversation among the children.
•	 Make worksheets with pictures of objects starting with different 
letters/aksharas, and ask them to colour the ones with the 
same beginning sound in identical colours.
•	 Make worksheet with pictures of the objects and let children 
write the letters or aksharas, with which the name of the object 
starts. 
•	 Let the children guess. For example, how many words can they 
think of, with the beginning sound ‘m’. 
•	 Let the children identify the beginning sound of a picture and 
circle the letter or akshara.
•	 Show flash cards of a few words with the same phonemic sound 
(bat, ball, balloon, etc.), and ask them to identify the beginning 
letter and think of more words with that letter or akshara.
Other competencies 
addressed
Physical and motor development; socio-emotional development; 
positive learning habits; knowing words to carry out day to day 
interactions; understands oral instructions; differentiates between 
shapes, sounds, etc.
What can be observed?
Observe that children sing rhymes/songs, mimic and reproduce 
syllabic sounds, identify rhyming words, beginning and end 
syllables, combine syllables to form simple words, combine sounds 
to form familiar words.
Activity 2
Blending and Segmenting
Material required
Storybook, newspaper, pencil, worksheets.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Let the children sit in a circle. 
•	 Make a sound like ‘la...la...la...’ in a particular tune and then 
extend it with the children and say ‘ma..ma..ma...’.
•	 Children must be given a chance to hear different syllables 
informally and  from there, they must be encouraged to make 
words like mala, baja, aaja, etc. 


Vigyanmaya Kosha
223
4 – 5 years
•	 Let the children sit in a circle. Ask them to clap twice and click 
their fingers twice. Let them enjoy and practice two-three times. 
Then select any word like ‘cat’ and ask them to make rhyming 
words  like ‘bat’, ‘rat’, etc. 
Let them make and speak 
rhyming words while clicking 
their fingers so that their 
voices can be heard. 
•	 Distribute letter cards to 
the children. Show a picture 
to them, like a picture of a 
cat. Now let the child with 
letter ‘c’ come forward’, while 
coming forward ask all the children in the class to identify and 
make the sound of the letter, then the child with letter ‘a’ comes 
forward. Similarly, the child with the ‘t’ comes forward’. Let the 
children blend and say in a loud voice ‘cat!’
•	 They may also be asked to act as or make the sound of a cat. 
After completion of the activity, let the children move apart, e.g., 
‘cat’ is segmented into three different phonemes (sounds): /c/, 
/a/, and /t/ and let them go back to their positions.
5 – 6 years
Play treasure hunt. Hide the letters or aksharas in the four corners. 
Show a picture to the children and ask them to look for the letter 
or akshara with which the name of the picture starts. Similarly, 
show another picture and let the children go again and look for 
the letters or aksharas forming the name of the picture, and in 
this manner the game continues. For example, show the picture of 
कमल and hide ‘क’, ‘म’, ‘ल’ in different corners of the room. Let the 
children find and arrange the aksharas to form a word.
Variation
Let the children stand in a circle. Distribute commonly used 
letters/aksharas to them. Place one picture in the centre and let 
all the children look at it. Beat the dafli. When you beat the dafli, 
those children carrying letters forming words will come forward 
and make words like कमल/ कलम.
Other competencies 
addressed
Physical 
motor 
development; 
socio-emotional 
development; 
cognitive development; explores different sounds and aksharas to 
form new words; follows instructions; development of self esteem, 
self expression.
What can be observed?
Observe if the children can identify the phonemes of the words, 
either the beginning letter, the middle letter, or the ending letter. 


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
224
C–10.2: Understands basic structure/format of a book, the idea of 
words in print and direction in which 
they are printed, and recognises basic 
punctuation marks 
Activity 
Print has Meaning
Material required
Picture book with words, colour pencils, chalks, worksheets, 
wrappers.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Collect some wrappers and display them in the class. Let the 
children identify the wrappers. Free conversation may continue 
about the objects packed in these wrappers.
•	 Provide opportunity to the children to observe you while 
handling the book or the newspaper. Be a role model to them.
•	 Let them pay attention on handling of books with pictures.
•	 Let them imitate their peers, or the teacher while reading a 
book. For example, the child flips the page, moves the index 
finger on the print, and repeats simple words after the teacher/
caregiver.
Variation
•	 Ask the children to copy and imitate their parents/siblings, e.g., 
how their mother reads newspapers, how their elder brother or 
sister read their lessons, or write in their notebooks.
4 – 5 years
•	 Make small groups of children and give each group a picture 
book. Ask the children to find the picture of a particular animal/
bird in it and show it to the class and let all other children clap. 
Similarly, the activity can continue for other pictures.
Variation
•	 Narrate a story using a children’s storybook. While narrating a 
story, ask the children what they think will happen next, e.g., 
Who will win— the tortoise or the hare?
5 – 6 years
•	 Make a placard with a question mark symbol. During the free 
conversation time ask them a creative question like, ‘What do 
you think will happen next?’.
•	 You can organise question time/riddle time and use a placard 
with a question mark simultaneously. Later, during the 
designated time, show them the placard with the question mark 
and ask them, ‘What will Raju do now?’


Vigyanmaya Kosha

Variation
•	 Narrate a story through pictures with pauses, facial expression 
and voice modulation. Gradually, they will learn to associate 
pause with a full stop.
•	 Organise a kathputli show for storytelling.
•	 Display storybooks in the class with cover pages having cues to 
the content. Show the cover page and ask the children to guess 
the story. 
Other competencies 
addressed
Creative and aesthetic development; socio-emotional development;

Observe each child's gesture of holding the book, flipping pages, 
moving her this finger across the text, following words from left to 
right and from top to bottom, speaking about the book, etc.
C–10.3: Recognises all the letters of the alphabet (forms of akshara) 
of the script (LI) and uses this knowledge to read and write 
words
Activity 
Identifying Aksharas/Alphabets
Material required
Letter cards, pictures, sensory bags.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Provide pictures to the children. 
•	 Sing a song about the akshara. Let the children match sounds 
with the pictures. It the teacher calls out ‘क’, the children have 
to pick the picture of a ‘कबूतर’.
4 – 5 years
•	 Make akshara cards. Let the children identify the akshara with 
which their name starts. Ask the children to come forward and 
form a circle. Those children whose name starts with the same 
akshara can be asked to sing a song together, and all the other 
children can follow. 
•	 Let the children write letters of the alphabet/akshara on a 
paper. Make this activity interesting by making them write with 
the finger dipped in colour or with crayons of different colours 
or by joining the dots.

in the bag. Let the

C–10.4: Reads stories and passages (in L1) with accuracy and fluency

Story Reading Activity

UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
228
C–10.5: Reads short stories and comprehends its meaning—by 
identifying characters, storyline and what the author wants 
to say—on their own (L1) 
Activity 1
Narration of Stories
Material required
Storybook, picture storybook, checklist for assessment.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Let all the children sit close to you. Tell them a short story. 
•	 Let them listen and ask a riddle on the main character, for 
example, “I say quack, quack, quack. Who am I?” 
•	 Let them look at the picture book and identify the character 
from the sound and the action.
4 – 5 years
•	 Let the children participate in the teacher-guided shared reading 
activity through picture cards and discuss their experience of 
reading the story. Also provide them an opportunity to have a look 
into various picture books and identify the characters and plots.
•	 Let them also narrate the story in sequence by using their own 
words.
•	 Provide picture cards (based on a story), jumble them up and 
ask the children to arrange them in sequence of the story. 
•	 Organise a puppet show once a week.
5 – 6 years
•	 Let the children participate in guided reading sessions and 
discuss their reading. 
•	 Let them read aloud simple one line stories by using visual cues 
and text to infer and retell the story in sequence and elaboration.
Variation
Let the children make storybooks by drawing or colouring/tearing 
and pasting activity, etc.
Other competencies 
addressed
Creative and aesthetic development; boost imagination; positive 
learning habits; socio-emotional development; develop self 
expresssion, development of self esteem.
What can be observed?
Observe the children’s participation in the class; observe whether 
they listen to the story and are able to retell in their own words, 
participate in shared/guided reading, tell the details of the picture 
in their own words.


Vigyanmaya Kosha
229
Activity 2
Discussion Beyond the Story
Material required
Storybooks, few everyday objects, sheets with animal/bird drawings.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Provide storybooks (mainly pictorial) to the children. Let the 
children make real-life connections by identifying their favourite 
objects or pictures in the storybooks.
•	 Encourage them to look into the pictures, build their own story 
and let them give alternate names to the characters.
4 – 5 years
•	 Read a story to the children with pictures and have a free 
conversation with them.
•	 Ask them which character they like the most and why.
Variation
•	 Let the children sit in a circle. The teacher pins a picture of 
an animal/bird on the back of a child’s shirt/dress without 
showing him/her. 
•	 Let the child go round the circle with his/her back towards the 
other children so that they can see the picture. 
•	 The child has to guess what is in the picture on his/her back 
by asking the other children for clues, for example, ‘Is it an 
animal?’ ‘Does it have  big ears?’ etc. 
•	 When the child gets the correct answer, all the children clap.
5 – 6 years
•	 Above activities for the age group 4 – 5 year can also be conducted 
for this age group.
•	 To ensure connections between the book and real life, the 
teacher/caregiver asks the child to think. The teacher/ caregiver 
reads the beginning of the story, for example,‘The monkey and 
the crocodile’, and comments —“This book reminds me of the 
time when my father took me to a zoo. Have you ever been to a 
zoo?” The children will be able to narrate their experiences too.
Variation
The teacher displays a few everyday objects on the table. The 
children take turns for picking up any one object and using it as a 
prop to enact a story character, for example, umbrella. The child 
acts using umbrella.
Other competencies 
addressed
Boosts imagination; narration; creativity; cooperation; can hold 
meaningful conversation; vocabulary building; self expression; 
social development.
What can be observed?
Observe children’s response/s to the open-ended questions, 
explanations in complete sentences, imagination, and narration, 
etc.

C–10.6: Reads short poems and begins to appreciate the poem for its

Activity 1

Vigyanmaya Kosha
231
Activity 2
Reciting Poem
Material required
Collection of poems.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Recite the poem, and then ask the children to repeat the poem and 
recite with actions and expressions.
4 – 5 years
•	 Recite a familiar poem, e.g., ‘Twinkle Twinkle Little Star…’. 
Make them understand the meaning of the words like ‘twinkle’, 
‘above’ by doing actions with hands/concrete examples. 
•	 Let the children explain the meaning and extend your 
conversation about pre-number concepts above-below, in-out, 
etc. Tell some other words that mean the same as twinkle. 
•	 Recite the poem, e.g., ‘मछली जल की रानी है’ and ask the children 
the meaning of the word ‘जल’; Let chidren say ‘जल’ means water. 
Then ask children what do they call ‘water’ in their language.
5 – 6 years
Recite a familiar poem and ask the children to explain it in their 
own words; also ask them to tell one thing which they liked the 
most in the poem.
Variation
Read poems a few times. Repeat the poems whenever you have 
time, or use poems like filler activity or for transitions, stops in 
between, and ask the children to say the next line.
Other competencies 
addressed
Emotional development; creative development; socio-emotional 
development; classroom participation; development of self 
esteem, self expression, communication skills, ability to engage in 
meaningful conversation.
What can be observed?
Observe the children’s response/s for repeating the poem with 
proper pronunciation, use of vocabulary, and expression. Observe 
the children’s understanding of word meaning, and the explaination 
of the meaning; poem recitation with intonation, voice modulation, 
expressions, gestures, etc.

Activity 3
Rhyming
Material required
Poetry book, pencil.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Recite the poem and ask the children to identify the rhyming 
words from the poem, e.g., makadi, lakdi and make new words 
like kakdi, etc. 
•	 Initially allow them to make meaningless words and slowly  and

4 – 5 years

poem further.

making expressions as expressed by the character.

addressed
Develops socio-emotional skills; creativity; cognitive development;

What can be observed?
Observe the children’s response/s for identifying the rhyming

expressions, etc. 


Vigyanmaya Kosha
233
C–10.7: Reads and comprehends the meaning of short news items, 
instructions and recipes, and publicity material (L1) 
Activity 1
Let’s Cook
Material required
Recipe book with simple instructions and pictures, worksheet, 
menu card printout, ingredients for making a sandwich.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Show a chart to the children, e.g., a chart showing 3 steps 
of making  a recipe like a sandwich. Use real food items and 
demonstrate the process by following the instructions from the 
chart, and countinue to have free conversation about the food 
items being used with them. 
•	 Encourage the children to prepare a sandwich at home with the 
help of their family members. Let them identify the ingredients 
and tell them what to do. Follow the steps shown in the chart 
with the children. For example, the children identify bread, 
butter/jam, and spoon/knife to make a sandwich; then he/she 
repeats the instructions to make a sandwich at home.
4 – 5 years
•	 The above activity can be conducted for this group as well.
•	 Give jumbled pictures of ingredients for a recipe. The children 
will identify the sequence of the recipe. For example, in 
the jumbled pictures of ingredients that are used to make 
sandwiches, the child identifies the first picture used to start 
making the sandwich, which is bread, then identifies the butter 
and the filling, and finally the other piece of bread.
5 – 6 years
•	 Preparing a sandwich. Divide the children into small groups. 
Give each group a recipe chart with simple step-by-step 
instructions to read and follow.
•	 You may ask the children while moving from one group to 
another like — ‘What are the ingredients for the recipe?’, ‘What 
is the first step?’, ‘What to do next?’, etc.
Variation
You may encourage children to think what else they can make on 
their own.
Other competencies 
addressed
Develops fine motor skills; language development; cognitive 
development; follows oral instruction; enhances vocabulary; 
improves sequential thinking.
What can be observed?
Observe the children’s participation, comprehension skills, 
sequential thinking, etc.

Activity 2
Using Newspaper to Identify Letters of 
the  Alphabet
Material required
Newspapers, colour pencils, hygiene realted posters.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Provide old newspapers to children. Let them observe the

•	 Divide the children into small groups and give a newspaper to 
each group. Provide them crayons. 
•	 Let the children draw the outline of pictures, scribble on the 
newspaper, turn pages, arrange papers one after the other, etc.

4 – 5 years
Provide old newspapers or magazines to children. Ask them to 
identify letters/aksharas used in the titles and circle them.
Variation
Show a scene or picture used in a newspaper and ask the children 
to explain with examples what the picture says, what may be the 
title of the picture, what message is conveyed, etc.
5 – 6 years
•	 Divide the children into small groups. Assign each group a letter 
of the alphabet/akshara. 
•	 Let the children browse through the newspaper, find five 
familiar or unfamiliar words beginning with the assigned letter/
akshara, and look for the meaning of the words by asking the 
teacher/caregiver a question. 
•	 Let one child from every group tell the selected words. The 
teacher/caregiver explains the meaning of the words to the class.
Variation
Ask each child to search in the newspaper through play activity, 
for example, any letter, location/place, any sport, word with seven 
letters, words starting with ‘p’, weather related pictures like sun/
moon/rain, advertisement of products, etc. 


Vigyanmaya Kosha
235
Other competencies 
addressed
Development of positive learning habits, socio-emotional and 
cognitive development; vocabulary building; enhances creativity; 
comprehension shells ; follows oral instructions.
What can be observed?
Observe each child's response/s for identifying capital letters, 
numbers, and pictures, child’s comprehension of newspaper 
content, vocabulary building, word meaning, etc., understanding 
of what the picture says, the title, the message they understood, 
etc.

Activity 1

Material required
Stationery
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years

Let the children make a greeting card with a message, e.g., ‘Happy 
Diwali’, etc.
4 – 5 years

newspapers or magazines. 
•	 Seeing the images, let the children also create their own picture 
book on how they celebrate festivals with messages like Happy 
Diwali, Merry Christmas, Eid Mubarak, etc., related to their 
creations.
5 – 6 years
Ask  the children to write a few lines describing how they celebrated 
the festival with drawings and display these in the classroom, and 
then put it in their portfolio.
Variation
Divide the children into small groups and ask the children to 
create posters for the festival—Diwali: giving messages for unity, 
happiness, caution against crackers.
Other competencies 
addressed
Develops creativity; socio-emotional development; addresses 
diversity and cultural integration; language development.
What can be observed?
•	 Children’s participation in creative artwork; observe their 
sharing, caring and cooperative behaviour. 
•	 Observe their creativity in drawing, colouring, verbal expression, etc.

C–10.9: Shows interest in picking up and reading
a variety of children’s books (L1) 
Activity 1
Book Reading
Material required
Storybook, worksheet, pencil.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Provide opportunity to select any one book from the reading 
area. See that the child selects an unfamiliar book with pictures.
•	 He/she then demonstrates interest in the picture book and 
reads through the pages, asking questions like— ’Who is this?’, 
‘What is on his face?’, etc.
Variation
•	 You can show them a photo album of a family.
4 – 5 years
•	 Ask the child to select one storybook with pictures, from the 
reading area. Ensure that they make an attempt to read the 
book with the support of pictures. 
•	 Provide opportunity to interact with peers about the book, 
look at the pictures together with friends and talk about the 
story; see that the children learn unfamiliar words, meaning of 
different phrases, etc. 
Variation
•	 Let the children paste pictures and make a picture book.
5 – 6 years
•	 Let the children read age-appropriate books with the support 
of pictures and after the child reads a book, he/she retells the 
story. 
•	 Ask questions to know whether the child has understood the 
story well, which character in the story he/she has liked the 
most, and why?
Variation
Let the children select a book of their choice and make a reading 
area in the class/home.
Other competencies 
addressed
Socio-emotional development; cognitive development; positive 
learning habits; imagination; develops sequential thinking.
What can be observed?
Observe each child's interest in the book by his/her expressions, 
words, and body language, and the responses the child makes 
to close-ended questions; how well the child retells a story after 
reading it, and answers the questions.

How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years

what will happen next!” or “Do you know who is a woodcutter?”

UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
240
Activity 3
Read Along
Material required
Storybook, worksheet, pencil/crayon
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Read aloud along with the child. Link the stories from the picture 
book to real-life experiences, and use what they know already to 
make sense out of the stories.
4 – 5 years
•	 Select a book from the reading area. 
•	 Divide children into pairs and ask them to look into the book 
together and share with each other what they see. 
•	 They begin to recognise different literary forms (e.g., rhymes, 
poems, stories, brochures, etc.) and share their understanding 
with each other. 
5 – 6 years
Let the children select a book of their choice. After reading, ask 
them, ‘Did you like the book?’, ‘What did you like the most?’ 
continue to ask, “What if ” questions like “What would happen if 
he was not honest?”, “What if the crocodile could talk?”
Variation
•	 Provide ample opportunities to observe pictures, read one line 
sentences with the support of pictures. 
•	 Make the environment in the classroom print rich. 
•	 Label all the objects in the classroom.
Other competencies 
addressed
Creative and aesthetic development; socio-emotional development; 
cognitive development; positive learning habits.
What can be observed?
Observe the child’s participation in the book reading area, use of 
drawing tools to express his/her ideas.


GOAL
11
C–11.1: Develops phonological awareness and able to blend 
phonemes/syllables into words, and segment words into 
phonemes/syllables 
Activity
Phoneme Games
Material required
Flash cards, letter cards, words, stories, fables.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
(Identification of 
sounds)
•	 Let the children sit in a semi-circle. Provide them opportunity 
to listen to the sounds in the environment like the sound of a 
fan, dog, transport, etc., and describe what they heard in their 
own language. 
•	 Let children recite poems/rhymes involving sound. 
•	 Start with children’s own names and help them learn to identify 
the beginning sound of their own name.
•	 Provide children opportunity to listen to rhymes, poems, songs, 
etc., and let them recite/sing and enjoy.
Variation
•	 Make flash cards for various objects, related to fruits/vegetables/
transport, etc. Show flash cards to children and let them know 
the names of the objects/items shown, to develop vocabulary. 
(Pictures should be used when actual things are not available/
possible to bring to class).
•	 Provide a print-rich environment.
•	 In the reading area, provide the opportunity to see picture books.
CG–11: Children Begin to Read and Write
in Language 2 (L2)
The activities have been developed with an assumption that the second 
language in Balvatika may be English.
•	 Opportunities to listen
•	 Oral language development
•	 Emergent reading
•	 Emergent writing
•	 Bonding with books


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
242
4 – 5 years
(Identification of 
beginning sound)
•	 Play the game of classification, for example, ask all the children 
whose name starts with a particular sound to come to one side. 
This will help in identifying a particular sound. 
•	 Let the children think of objects within the class with one beginning 
sound say ‘b’, they may identify balloon, bag, book, etc. This will 
help in building both sound discrimination and vocabulary.
•	 Provide a worksheet with pictures of some objects on it with 
some pictures starting with a similar sound and ask them to 
talk about it and colour.
•	 Say a sound and ask the children to pick up the card related to 
that sound. Let the children learn to associate the sound with a 
letter, e.g., sound of ब with B.
•	 Areas can be marked with letters of the alphabet say a sound of 
any related letter and let the children run to that area. Make it 
interesting for children. 
Variation
•	 Provide opportunity to listen to the words and relate them with 
real objects. Make words using letter of the alphabet cards.
•	 Make picture cards of letters of the alphabet and use them to 
make the learning interesting. 
•	 Make flash cards of blending words, e.g., bat, cat, dog, pen etc. 
•	 Make flash cards of blends of one consonant and one vowel 
sounds, e.g., ba, ca, do, pe, etc. and help children to make words.
•	 Create a print-rich environment.
•	 In the reading area, provide opportunity to see and comprehend 
storybooks having big pictures.
5 – 6 years
•	 Make flashcards for simple words (site words) and familarise 
the children with them e.g., my, he, she, it, etc.
•	 Let the children make rhyming words like bat, cat, mat, rat, etc.
•	 Let the children mimic and reproduce the words.
•	 Let the children identify what’s in the picture, and then break 
that word into its individual sound, e.g., dog is d-o-g, three 
sounds (phonemes). Create a print-rich environment.
•	 Help the children recognise simple words and read them 
independently. Narrate simple stories/fables. 
Variation
•	 In the reading area, provide opportunity to see and comprehend 
big books through picture reading, graded book series, etc.
•	 Use opportunities whenever you get to initiate new talk/
introduce new rhymes, etc., for every occasion, example – 
during play time, lunch time, nature walk time, etc.
•	 Encourage children to help each other in learning new words/
rhyming words, etc.
Other competencies 
addressed
Team work (helping other team members), leadership quality, 
including values; positive learning habits; focus attention; develop 
memorisation, observation, exploration, creativity.
What can be observed
Observe if children recognise, recall related words to the phonemes; 
help each other and relate to the team; equally participate in group 
work; comprehend simple instructions, texts, etc.

able to  differentiate between ‘p’ and ‘b’, ‘b’ and ‘d’ and so on.


Creativity, bliss (joyfulness), fearlessness, love, beauty, 
sense of joy are the basic elements of Anandamaya 
Kosha. A child is naturally creative and blissful, loving and 
fearless. Activities that enhance these qualities, develop 
Anandamaya Kosha. Activities like games, creative art 
work, music, dance, drawing, colouring,  role-play acting, 
etc., fall under the umbrella of Anandamaya Kosha.
Anandamaya Kosha
(Creative and Aesthetic Development)


Curricular Goals Related to
Anandamaya Kosha
CG–12  Children develop abilities and sensibilities in visual 
and performing arts, and express their emotions 
through art in meaningful and joyful ways.


ANANDAMAYA KOSHA
GOAL
12
Anandamaya kosha is the condition of happiness, when children are so engrossed 
in their activity that they forget about their surroundings — they are in the realm of 
anandamaya kosha. Any type of creation and innovation that instills joyfulness comes 
under this domain. Also, aesthetic senses should not be limited to visuals only. It should 
be expanded to the magic of sounds and music, taste, smell and touch. Development of 
all five senses, (e.g., listen, taste, touch, smell, sight) is important.
CG–12: Children Develop Abilities and Sensibilities in 
Visual and Performing Arts, and Express their 
Emotions Through Art in Meaningful and Joyful 
Ways
C–12.1: Explores and plays with a variety of materials and tools to 
create two-dimensional and three-dimensional artworks in 
varying sizes
Activity 1
Draw, Colour and Paint 
Material required
Old newspaper, white paper, slate, coloured chalks.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years 
•	 Provide the children with crayons and an old newspaper or any 
other paper and ask them to scribble/draw on the paper. 
•	 You may provide a slate and let them draw with coloured chalk 
on it. Large brushes and paint can be provided to children to 
paint what they like. 
•	 Do not impose your ideas on the children, let them draw or 
paint what they like and the way they like to do it. 
•	 Appreciate even the smallest efforts.
CREATIVE AND AESTHETIC DEVELOPMENT 


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
248
Variation
•	 Provide the children with some wet mud and give them a short 
stick or stem. They can scribble or draw on mud/make toys/
shapes from mud.
•	 Large circle/triangle/square can be drawn on a paper/the 
ground in the form of a rangoli and children can be asked to 
colour within the shape with crayons.
•	 Make a big rangoli with the children using different material.
4 – 5 years
•	 Draw the outline of any shape or any picture and ask the 
children to colour with crayons or natural colours. 
•	 You may give a familiar theme to the children and ask them to 
draw and colour. Colours from natural resources, e.g., mehndi 
leaves, flowers, leaves, sand, grains can be used. 
•	 Give painting brushes with water colours to children for free 
hand drawing.
Variation
Give a theme to the children and ask them to draw, e.g., A Rainy 
Day, A Sunny Day.
5 – 6 years
•	 Give the children crayons and colours and let them use these 
freely to create their own drawings and paintings. You may also 
provide pictures to colour with increased complexity. 
•	 Let them fill colours within the given outlines. Themes can be 
given and the children can be asked to draw scenes. 
Variation
Children can do spray-painting by dipping old toothbrushes in 
water colour and using the bristles of the brush to spray the colour. 
Other competencies 
addressed
Develops eye-hand coordination, emotional development, positive 
self concept, builds imagination.
What can be observed?
Observe the children’s colouring in an enclosed space, their 
attention span, creativity in creating simple pattern, their art 
work, collage, imprints, indulgence, joyfulness, mindfulness.


Anandamaya Kosha
249
Activity 2
Tear, Cut and Paste
Material required
Newspaper, origami paper, glue, scissor, old magazine, etc.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Provide the children with some waste paper; for example, pages 
from old magazines, newspapers, etc. 
•	 Draw an outline of an animal or an object on a large piece of 
paper. The outline should be large and spacious. 
•	 Put glue on it and ask children to tear pieces of paper given 
to them and paste the pieces within the outline. The outline 
could be that of a tree, a hut, a ball, a cat, etc. Let the children 
decorate a gift box with tearing and pasting activity.
4 – 5 years
•	 Give the children simple shapes like a square or a triangle 
drawn on a paper and get them to cut paper neatly along the 
outline given using child friendly scissors. 
•	 Give them pages of old newspapers, magazines, etc., and 
encourage them to make their own collage, by pasting the pieces 
in any design they want to create.
Variation
•	 On a piece of paper, let the children draw any shape. Spread 
glue on it and let them sprinkle sand on the shape, and let it 
dry. An embossed design will emerge.
•	 Provide colour to make imprints of objects like leaves, blocks, 
palm imprints, etc., on old newspaper or cloth.
5 – 6 years
•	 Give the children pages of old newspapers, magazines, etc., 
and encourage them to make their own collage, by pasting the 
pieces in any design they want to create. They could be given 
old cloth pieces also and other waste material/ leaves/ petals, 
etc., which they could use for pasting in the collage work.
•	 Ask them to tear paper into small pieces, thereafter, paste the 
torn paper within a given outline. The outline could be that of a 
tree, a hut, an animal, or any other object. 
•	 Let them make their own collage by pasting the pieces in any 
design they want to create. 
Variation
Provide clay material to make objects of their choice.
Other competencies 
addressed
Socio-emotional development; eye-hand coordination; increased 
attention span; perseverance on task.
What can be observed?
Learns the use of art material, tools and instruments; makes new 
patterns; eye-hand coordination; joyfulness; mindfulness/aware 
of  what they are doing.

Activity 3
Paper Folding
Material required
Paper, bedsheet, handkerchief, napkin, towel.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Ask the children to bring a handkerchief. Let them fold their 
handkerchiefs using one or two folds in different shapes. Papers 
can also be folded to create caps, boats, etc. 
Variation
Let the children draw water on a sheet of paper and make fishes 
by folding origami paper, and then pasting it in the water to create 
a collage/scene.
4 – 5 years
Children can be taught to make different objects like a cap, 
bird, ice-cream, etc., which require simple paper folding. Provide 
opportunity to fold bedsheets/sitting mats by joining the corners.
Variation
Draw a garden scene and guide the children to make flower using 
paper folding (origami work), and ask them to paste the flowers to 
complete the garden scene.
5 – 6 years
Provide colourful papers to the children. They can be taught 
complex paper-folding to make a boat, a bird, an airplane, etc.
Variation
•	 Ask the children to use their paper folding creations and make 
various sceneries.
•	 Put the work of the children in their portfolio and observe their 
progress.
Other competencies 
addressed
Develop eye-hand coordination; fine motor skills; creative and 
aesthetic development.
What can be observed?
Children’s 
participation, 
creativity, 
eye-hand 
coordination; 
children creating a variety of visual imagery, sharing their own 
ideas, observing local culture, enjoying their creation.

Activity 4
Clay Work
Material required
Wooden board or cardboard, colours, painting brush, twigs, 
flowers, etc.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Provide dough or clay to children. Let them beat or pat the clay and 
ask them to make anything of their choice like balls of different 
sizes, chappatis, etc.
Variation
Give mud to the children and ask them to make a rope and balls 
using mud, and let them dry it in the sun. Then paint with colours 
and use them in making of toys like a snake, cart, etc.
4 – 5 years
•	 Give a wooden board or cardboard to each child and give them 
a lump of clay for molding into simple shapes like a diya, glass, 
bowl, etc. 
•	 Let the molded objects dry. Once dry, paint the molded object. 
Variation
Let the children make toys using mud or clay and play with them.
5 – 6 years
•	 Provide the children with some sand, clay or dough and tell 
them to make objects of their choice with it. Give them some 
ideas. 
•	 Let the children use twigs, flowers, colour, etc., to decorate their 
creation.
Variation
•	 Let the children make wheels, dry them and colour them and 
make a cart using sticks, wheels and string. 
•	 Let them pull it and dramatise as if they are transporting objects 
from one place to another using their cart.
Other competencies 
addressed
Fine motor development skills; eye-hand coordination; language 
skills; social development.
What can be observed?
Creativity, precision and control, coordination, cooperation, 
expression of joy/emotions.

Activity 2
Cat and Mouse Game
Material required
Mask of a cat, mouse.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 6 years
Let one child be a cat and the others mice. Put a mask on the child’s 
forehead. The cat must try to catch the mice. All the children must 
run here and there to escape from the cat. The children can sing, 
‘chuha bhaag bili aaee.’


Anandamaya Kosha
253
Variation
•	 Let the children make a circle by holding hands. Make one child 
a cat and another child a mouse. Let the mouse be inside the 
circle and the cat outside the circle. The cat must try to catch 
the mouse by trying to come inside the circle, but the other 
children must not allow the cat to enter the circle and catch the 
mouse. 
•	 The children can take turns to become the cat and the mouse. 
Let them perform body movements, role-play and dramatisation.
Other competencies 
addressed
Develop gross motor skills; cooperations; team work, voice 
modulation, imagination.
What can be observed?
Observe team spirit, adjustment in a group, cooperation, mimics 
with joyfulness and vigour.
C–12.3: Innovates and works imaginatively to express ideas and 
emotions through the art 
Activity 1
Let’s Dance
Material required
Dholak, Dhafli
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 6 years
•	 Play a dholak or dafli and introduce a beat. Increase or decrease 
the beats at time intervals, by ensuring variation in speed, 
rhythm and notes. 
•	 Let the children dance or walk, or act according to the beats. 
Make one child the leader. Ask the leader to change the actions 
now and then, and others must follow the leader.
Variation
Let the children make different body sounds like foot stamping, 
clapping, laughing, etc., in accordance to the sound or sing while 
playing the musical instruments, and ask the children to dance to 
the tunes. Let them dance freely as they wish.
Other competencies 
addressed
Gross motor skills; fine motor skills; language development; socio-
emotional development.
What can be observed?
Observe 
children’s 
participation, 
expressions, 
initiation, 
leadership, and emotions.


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
254
Activity 2
Dramatic Play
Material required
Full length mirror, dupattas, hat, purse, tie, doctor’s set, kitchen 
set, tools, etc.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 6 years
•	 Keep the items like a full length mirror, dupattas, hat, purse, 
tie, doctor’s set, kitchen set, tools, etc., in the dramatic play 
area. 
•	 Watch the children become a doctor, shopkeeper, teacher, etc. 
Give some directions or cues like ‘walk like an elephant in a 
jungle’, ‘fly like a butterfly in the garden’, ‘act like a doctor seeing 
a patient’, ‘be a teacher teaching her class’, etc. One group of 
children enact and the others guess who they are.
Other competencies 
addressed
Creative movement; free expression; language development; socio-
emotional development.
What can be observed?
Imagination, socio-emotional development, language development, 
mimics a few recognisable people, animals, plants, objects, etc.
Activity 3
Guess the Action
Material required
Pencil, handkerchief, paper.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 6 years
•	 Take any object, for example, a pencil, imagining it to be 
something else. Mime an action with it, e.g., brushing of teeth. 
Let the children guess what you have imagined it to be. 
•	 Then give a chance to any other child of the class. He/she can 
mime another action e.g., imagining a pencil to be something 
quite different like a stirrer. 
•	 Thus the activity moves on and the children have to guess the 
imagined object from the actions.
Other competencies 
addressed
Develops 
imagination; 
problem 
solving 
skills; 
vocabulary 
development.
What can be observed
Observe how children enact, show the details in their action.


Anandamaya Kosha
255
C–12.4: Works collaboratively in the arts 
Activity 1
Rangoli Making
Material required
Petals, leaves, beads, coloured sand, twigs, etc.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 6 years
In a small group give the children petals of different colours, leaves, 
coloured sand, etc., and encourage them to make their own rangoli 
designs or fill the design given by the teacher.
Variation
They could be given old cloth pieces, beads and other waste 
materials like bottle caps, etc., which they can use for filling the 
rangoli designs.
Other competencies 
addressed
Develops 
cooperation; 
language 
development; 
cognitive 
development; socio-emotional development, etc.
What can be observed?
Children’s involvement, participation, cooperation, fine motor 
development, eye-hand coordination, indulgence, and mindfulness.
Activity 2
Festival Celebration
Material required
Clay, wool, twigs, colour chart paper, glue, stapler, etc.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 6 years
Celebrate all festivals in the school depending on the local, regional, 
and cultural context. Encourage the children to participate in the 
celebrations of festivals by making preparations for the festival.
Variation
Let the children make diyas; rakhis, a Christmas tree with tearing-
pasting activity and decorate it; visit friends during Festivals, etc.
Other competencies 
addressed
Develops imagination, vocabulary, aesthetic sense; creative 
expression, language development; socio-emotional development.
What can be observed?
Observe children’s participation, creativity, awareness of different 
festivals, acceptance, cooperation, unity.


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
256
C–12.5: Communicates and appreciates a variety of responses 
while creating and experiencing different forms of art, 
local culture, and heritage
Activity 1
Creating A Classroom Display Board
Material required
Bulletin board, stapler, pencil/crayon, markers, scissors, chart 
papers, scale, etc.
Age group
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 6 years
•	 Create classroom displays at the eye-level of the children. 
•	 Choose a theme (to introduce a concept, reinforce learning, 
decorate the class); make drawings/collage with children; 
arrange the drawings/pictures you would like to display by 
involving them. 
•	 Make the background and border of the board, and stick the 
pictures and words. 
Variation
Take any theme like air, water, fruits, vegetables etc. Make displays 
and change the displays as often as feasible, preferably according 
to the theme. Encourage children to verbalise their appreciation 
for their and others’ work. 
Other competencies 
addressed
Language development; spatial understanding; socio-emotional 
development.
What can be observed?
Observe children’s participation. Observe, if the child shares and 
appreciates the responses and ideas, responds to artwork verbally, 
shows sensitivity towards colours, beauty, etc.


Competencies for developing positive learning habits 
include, habit formation like regular attendance, 
punctuality, participation and involvement in class 
activities, waiting for one’s turn, respecting rights of others, 
listening to others, respecting others’ point of view, taking 
leadership, completing the task on time (persistence on 
task), learning to learn, etc.

DEVELOPING POSITIVE LEARNING HABITS
GOAL
13
C–13.1: Attention and intentional action: acquires skill to plan, 
focus attention, and direct activities to achieve specific 
goals
Activity 1
Explore and Enjoy
Material required
Blocks (wooden), empty boxes, sand, clay, rings, kitchen utensils 
like metal bowls, spoons, etc., instruments to play/create music, 
mirror.
Age group 
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Provide material (objects/toys/musical instruments) to the 
children and let them explore from the given material, and 
choose to create things of their choice by creating and recreating 
with minimal intervention and guidance from the teachers. 
•	 Keep books and let children explore the books on their own . 
•	 Provide blocks, empty boxes to create toys/objects of their 
choice.
•	 Provide clay for free exploration. 
Variation
•	 In the reading area, let the children explore picture books. 
Encourage them to read by looking at pictures, putting their 
finger beneath the pictures/words and let them draw, colour 
and paint.
•	 Children can explore dance and body movements using props 
like rings, ribbons, mirror, music, etc. 
CG–13: Children Develop Habits of Learning that Allow 
them to Engage Actively in Formal Learning 
Environments Like A School Classroom


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
260
4 – 5 years
•	 Let the children explore from the given material and choose to 
create things of their choice, by creating and recreating with 
minimal interference and guidance from the teachers.
•	 Let the children sit in a circle and keep some books with big 
pictures in the centre. Ask them to take a book of their choice 
and  look into the book and share what they are looking at with 
gestures.
Variation 
•	 Provide blocks, empty matchboxes. Let the children wrap them 
with paper, make furniture like a sofa, chair, etc., with them. 
•	 Provide clay for free exploration.
•	 Children can explore dance and movement to music using 
various props.
5 – 6 years
•	 Let the children explore from the given material and choose to 
create things of their choice, by creating and recreating with 
minimal intervention and guidance from the teachers. 
•	 Let them do independent work like creating things/
experimenting/reading, etc.
Variation
•	 Place a full length mirror in the class and let the children see 
themselves in the mirror. Play a music and let the children 
dance to the music looking at the mirror. 
•	 If a mirror is not available, let the children enjoy free-dance 
movements.
•	 Props like ribbons, scarfs can be used to enhance the dance to 
the rhythm of the music. 
•	 Make available a variety of picture books/drawing books, which 
the children can easily access and explore. 
•	 Provide opportunities to the children to take responsibility of 
simple tasks in the classroom. For example, let them distribute 
writing material, craft material to younger children/children in 
the same class, etc. 
Other competencies 
addressed 
Enjoyment in self-creation; imagination; body coordination; 
enjoying rhythm/beats/music; development of a positive self-
concept; sense of initiative; independence; leadership and 
autonomy.
What can be observed?
Observe the children who are open to explore, see how they 
make choices, stick to the chosen material or switch to different 
materials, management of disappointment and/or frustration.  


Developing Positive Learning Habits
261
Activity 2
Stimulating Imagination
Material required
List of age-appropriate stimulating questions which the teacher 
can keep ready by preparing in advance.
Age group 
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Ask the children questions which can stimulate their imagination, 
for example, ‘What would happen if a squirrel comes to your class?’
Variation
Encourage the children to come up with such questions and ask 
each other. 
4 – 5 years
Children can enact various situations/actions with the help of a 
bucket (different uses of bucket).
Variation 
•	 The above mentioned activity is to be played in 2 teams/groups. 
•	 Group 1 enacts the different uses of any object—like a 
handkerchief and the other team (group 2) can guess the use 
that is shown by the first group. 
5 – 6 years 
•	 Give a drawing to paint/colour; provide limited number of 
colours, other material from nature like leaves, etc., and let the 
children explore if they can come up with options to create more 
colours and paint the picture. 
•	 Each child shares by asking, moving around, looking for 
colours/available material and also exchanges colours and 
resources with other children to complete one’s painting.
Variation 
Let the children do thread painting and show their paintings to 
friends and ask them to look into the picture, and share with each 
other what they see.
Other competencies 
addressed 
Adaptation; flexibility, imagination; verbal expression; sharing of 
resources; offering and receiving help.
What can be observed?
Observe the children who show the skills of  adaptability in using 
the given material, ability to be flexible, imaginative, ability to 
verbally express ideas.


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
262
Activity 3
Statue
Material required
Musical instrument
Age group 
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years  
Play the music. Let the children move around when the music is 
played and stop moving their bodies when the music is paused. If 
they continue to move after the music is stopped, they are asked 
to sing a song or act like an elephant/butterfly, etc.
Variation 
Do some actions like clapping, clicking, etc. While you are doing 
the action, ask the children to sing la, la, la, la in a particular tone 
decided at the beginning of the game. When you stop the action, 
the children need to stop saying la, la, la, la.
4 – 5 years
Play music and allow the children to hop around. When the music 
stops, the children have to take a statue position—they need to 
freeze and not move.
Variation 
•	 After the first 2–3 rounds of the activity make it a little challenging. 
•	 Let one child make an attempt to make others laugh so as to 
disturb the statue position. One whose eyebrows, eyelids or 
body moves he/she will be called out in the centre and the game 
continues. 
5 – 6 years 
•	 Let a child in the class play the music, and the other children 
walk backward till the music is stopped. 
•	 When the music stops, the children must freeze like a statue; 
and when the music restarts, the children must immediately 
start walking forward. 
Variation 
From the above activity—change the ‘freeze like a statue’ with  a 
dance position. 
Other competencies 
addressed
Auditory attention; motor coordination; sensorial development; 
socialisation. 
What can be observed?
Observe the children who are attentive to music, who are quiet 
and stable while in a statue position. Observe those who are not 
calm and steady and encourage them to be in a statue position.


Developing Positive Learning Habits
263
C–13.2: Memory and mental flexibility: develops adequate working 
memory, mental flexibility (to sustain or shift attention 
appropriately) and self-control (to resist impulsive 
actions or responses) that would assist them 
in learning in structured environments
Activity 1
Traffic Rules
Material required
Bowl with chits, toy ball, dafli/drum, chalks. 
Age group 
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
•	 Traffic control: Create marked paths in the playground, for 
example—left turn, right turn, red light, green light. 
•	 Ask the children to walk/pretend to ride different vehicles by 
following traffic rules.
Variation
•	 Children may take turns to control the traffic in the class by 
showing the stop and go signs, the red and green light signs, etc. 
4 – 5 years
•	 Allow the children to freely run, jump in and around the drawn 
circle or walk till a particular line and not go beyond a particular 
line if a red sign is shown. 
•	 Let the children sing, dance if the green sign is shown. They 
have to slow down when the yellow sign is shown. 
Variation
Let the children enact the role of a traffic policeman. Ask them to 
recall and add activities done in the school. 
5 – 6 years
Take a bowl and keep few folded chits having some tasks written 
on it; for example, rhyme on traffice lights, names of transport, 
counting 1 to 20, letters in order, names of few fruits, vegetable, 
etc. Then give children a soft toy/ball. Play dafli/drum and ask 
the children to pass the toy/ball until the music is stopped. As the 
sound of the dafli stops, the child having a ball in his/her hand 
has to pick up the chit and perform the task as mentioned in the 
chit.
Other competencies 
addressed
Development of cognitive skills, sensorial perception; vocabulary 
development, motor coordination. 
What can be observed?
Notice the children who can control and follow the specifications 
in the activity, help those children who are not able to understand 
through concrete examples.


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
264
Activity 2
Collage Making
Material required
Big chart paper, drawing material, available natural things from 
the environment like leaves, pebbles, sticks, etc.
Age group 
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Collecting rocks/pebbles/shells/leaves individually and bringing 
them to the classroom. 
Variation 
Working in a group to arrange the collected things from the nature 
for display. 
4 – 5 years
Children can explore the readily available material around their 
surroundings like pebbles, leaves, sticks and come up with any 
creation of their choice.
Variation
•	 Children work in pairs, small groups, exchange or gather all the 
material they have to create a model/collage in a pair. 
•	 One child starts building a model, the second child adds on 
to it, the model or collage is completed in a small group by 
collectively taking turns.  
5 – 6 years
•	 Select one theme and give one big chart to a small group of 
children. Let all of them draw together on one big sheet. 
•	 Write their names and draw one symbol representing their name 
like anything of their choice say flower, fruit, sun, moon, star, etc.
Variation 
The child sees the drawing or symbol drawn by the first child and 
adds something else to the same drawing, the next child sees a 
drawing by another child and adds to it and so on. The additions 
are done without erasing/disturbing the original drawing.  
Other competencies 
addressed
Working in a group; understanding the rules of the games; 
interacting with each other, imagination; cognitive development, 
socialisation.
What can be observed?
Observe the children who can work collaboratively, who are patient 
while working and the children who cannot wait for their turns/
being impatient. Help children to be patient and wait for their turn.


Developing Positive Learning Habits
265
C–13.3: Observation, wonder, curiosity, and exploration: observes 
minute details of objects, wonders and explores by using 
various senses, tinkers with the objects and asks questions
Activity 1
Ask Questions
Material required
Available  natural materials, like leaves, sticks, pebbles, etc. 
Materials like lens, feather magnet, etc. 
Age group 
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Show a leaf and a flower and ask the children from where do we 
get these.
Variation
Collect leaf and any other available part—fruit/flower of the trees 
in the surroundings.
4 – 5 years
Show a drawing or photograph or object and ask the children to 
recognise example—pebbles, neem leaves, rose flower, etc. 
variation
Let a child stand in front and call out a name or show a picture 
of some object which is available in the class. For example  —  Who 
finds the white flower faster. 
5 – 6 years
•	 Let the children collect natural things form the surroundings 
and explore their features for example, let them rub two pebbles 
on each other and observe what happens or sense the smell of 
a crumpled leaf.
•	 Show them a leaf and ask them to find a tree in the garden to 
which the leaf belongs. Show them a flower and ask them to 
find the bush to which the flower belongs.
Variation 
Provide a variety of materials like lens, feather, magnet, etc., to the 
children. Let them use the material freely and explore. Ask them 
questions when they are playing.  What do you think? Where do 
we use it? How can we use it? What would happen if…? Let’s see 
if it works, etc.
Other competencies 
addressed
Develops imagination, exploration, stimulation, divergent thinking, 
sensorial development, language development.
What can be observed?
Observe the children who are coming up with many ideas, who 
take initiative to give response, observe children who are not 
readily coming to give a response, observe very novel responses, 
children who can think of many different innovative ideas.


UNMUKH — Trainers’ Handbook for Balvatika
266
C–13.4: Classroom norms: adapts and follows norms
with an understanding
Activity 1
Make and Follow Rules
Material required
Chart paper
Age group 
How to conduct the activity?
3 – 4 years
Follow the leader. Do simple actions in the class. Let the children 
follow the actions.
Variation 
Do fast actions, slow down and then stop. Let the children enjoy; 
for example, roll-roll your hand and suddenly stop.
4 – 5 years
Each child takes a turn in doing the actions and walking in the 
class, rest of the children follow the child in doing the actions. 
Variation 
•	 Let the children decide some rules for a day to follow. Example—
raise the left hand if you have to ask the teacher a question or 
if you want to talk in the class. 
•	 Spread your right hand out if you need to use the washroom. 
•	 Let the children observe if all of them are able to remember 
and follow the rules framed and help the child who forgets the 
appropriate action. 
5 – 6 years
•	 Play ‘Do as I say’. Ask the children to listen carefully and do the 
actions. You can do different actions, e.g., touch nose but say 
‘touch your ears’. 
•	 Children have to hold their ears but looking at you they may 
hold their nose. 
•	 Discuss with the children and  write the rules on the chart 
paper, draw visuals, put them on display boards and ensure 
that they are followed.
Variation
Make a chart for appropriate norms and help the children follow 
the rules, and give a star if the rules are followed as framed. Give a 
chart for home also, for, example ‘switch off lights and fans when 
not in use’. 
Other competencies 
addressed
Language development, cognitive development, socialisation.
What can be observed?
Observe children who are regular in following the instructions, 
and children who have understood the concept of rules. Help those 
children who are not able to follow rules.
